| P2 | Ch15 (CH15-11) | The I/O-complexity statement is ambiguous in sign and loose in units. | P3 | Ch15 (CH15-21) | The statement is true of the bottleneck (decode remains bandwidth-bound) but incomplete in a way that matters for the figure's own right-hand panel. | P1 | Chapter 2 — What Actually Happens During Inference (CH2-01) | The two thresholds are derived from incompatible batching models. The ~5-sequence threshold follows only if each sequence independently streams the full weight shard (no weight-read amortization): 5 × 17.5 GB / 25 ms ≈ 3.5 TB/s. The ~295 threshold follows from the roofline ridge with amortized weight reads: AI ≈ B FLOP/byte and ridge ≈ 295 FLOP/byte. Under amortized continuous batching, HBM utilization at 25 ms TPOT is ~17.5 GB / (25 ms × 3.35 TB/s) ≈ 21% and does not rise with batch size; under no amortization, the compute-bound threshold would be ~1,400 sequences, not ~295. | ch2:136 mixes models: ~5 = no amortization (5 x 17.5 GB/25 ms = 3.5 TB/s > 3.35); ~295 = amortized (AI ~ B FLOP/byte crosses ridge 295). Under amortization util = 17.5/(0.025 x 3.35 TB/s) = 21%, flat in B. | State the batching assumption explicitly. Under amortized weight reads, HBM utilization is independent of batch size at fixed TPOT; the step becomes bandwidth-limited only when TPOT approaches the weight-read time (~5.2 ms), not at ~5 sequences. Under no amortization, bandwidth saturates near B≈5 but compute binds near B≈1,400. | P1 | 26 (CH26-05) | The same 2.0 rps figure is used in two incompatible ways in the same paragraph. As a "conservative planning cap" it implies the short-prompt workload's true ceiling is HIGHER than 2.0 rps, so an offered load of 2.0 rps would sit at ρ < 1 and the queue would NOT be inflated.
